Signature matches c stdlib function\n * of the same name.\n *\n * @param c Character to output\n * @returns Character output (never fails so no EOF ever returned)\n */\nint putchar(int c);\n\n/**\n * Writes string to simulator out log.  Signature matches c stdlib function of\n * the same name.\n *\n * @param str String to output\n * @returns 0 always (never fails so no error)\n */\nint puts(const char *str);\n\n/**\n * Writes ASCII hex representation of number to simulator out log.\n *\n * @param h Number to output in hex\n */\nvoid puthex(uint32_t h);\n\n/**\n * Immediately halts the simulation\n */\nvoid sim_halt();\n\n/**\n * Enables/disables performance counters.  This effects mcycle and minstret as\n * well as the mhpmcounterN counters.\n *\n * @param enable if non-zero enables, otherwise disables\n */\nstatic inline void pcount_enable(int enable) {\n  // Note cycle is disabled with everything else\n  unsigned int inhibit_val = enable ? 0x0 : 0xFFFFFFFF;\n  // CSR 0x320 was called `mucounteren` in the privileged spec v1.9.1, it was\n  // then dropped in v1.10, and then re-added in v1.11 with the name\n  // `mcountinhibit`. Unfortunately, the version of binutils we use only allows\n  // the old name, and LLVM only supports the new name (though this is changed\n  // on trunk to support both), so we use the numeric value here for maximum\n  // compatibility.\n  asm volatile(\"csrw  0x320, %0\\n\" : : \"r\"(inhibit_val));\n}\n\n/**\n * Resets all performance counters.  This effects mcycle and minstret as well\n * as the mhpmcounterN counters.\n */\nvoid pcount_reset();\n\n/**\n * Enables timer interrupt\n *\n * @param time_base Number of time ticks to count before interrupt\n */\nvoid timer_enable(uint64_t time_base);\n\n/**\n * Returns current mtime value\n */\nuint64_t timer_read(void);\n\n/**\n * Set a new timer value\n *\n * @param new_time New value for time\n */\nvoid timecmp_update(uint64_t new_time);\n\n/**\n * Disables timer interrupt\n */\nvoid timer_disable(void);\n\n/**\n * Returns current global time value\n */\nuint64_t get_elapsed_time(void);\n\n/**\n * Enables/disables the instruction cache. INITIAL_VAL 0xDEADBEEF\n\n// Simple LFSR step function\nuint32_t next_val(uint32_t cur_val) {\n  uint32_t bottom_bit = cur_val & 0x1;\n  cur_val >>= 1;\n\n  if (bottom_bit) {\n    cur_val ^= test_b_run_time() {\n  uint32_t val = INITIAL_VAL;\n  uint32_t mismatches = 0;\n  uint32_t last_cycles = count_ones_zeros(val);\n\n  for (int i = 0; i < NUM_ITERATIONS; ++i) {\n    val = next_val(val);\n\n    uint32_t cycles = count_ones_zeros(val);\n    if (cycles != last_cycles) {\n      ++mismatches;\n    }\n\n    last_cycles = cycles;\n  }\n\n  return mismatches;\n}\n\n// Run \"\n        \"disabled: 0x\");\n    puthex(mismatches);\n    return 1;\n  }\n\n  set_dit(1);\n  mismatches = test_b_run_time();\n  if (mismatches != 0) {\n    puts(\"FAILURE: Cycle mismatches seen for Here are the key characteristics:\n\n## Functionality\nThe predictor determines whether an instruction is a branch or jump and calculates its target address. As stated in the documentation, it \"will always predict taken\" for jumps, while for branches it \"will predict taken if the PC offset is negative.\"\n\n## Supported Instructions\nThe module handles both standard and compressed RISC-V instructions:\n- **Uncompressed**: JAL (jumps) and BRANCH (conditional branches)\n- **Compressed**: C.J, C.JAL (jumps) and C.BEQZ, C.BNEZ (conditional branches)\n\n## Design Characteristics\n- **Combinational logic**: The predictor operates purely through combinatorial assignments with no sequential logic\n- **Immediate extraction**: The module extracts and sign-extends various immediate formats (J-type, B-type, CJ-type, CB-type)\n- **Target calculation**: The predicted PC is computed as `fetch_pc_i + branch_imm`\n\n## Input/Output\n- Accepts 32-bit instruction data and current program counter\n- Outputs a prediction signal and target address\n\nThe implementation includes assertions to verify instruction type validation during fetch cycles.\n\n[Binary content (application/octet-stream, 3.3KB) also saved to /home/builder/.claude/projects/-app/adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32/tool-results/webfetch-1777901627980-c6srqi.bin]"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32","uuid":"7a6f5805-18e4-45e8-b3e3-02fe5e467139","timestamp":"2026-05-04T13:33:51.725Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":3351,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Branch Predictor Module Overview\n\nThis Verilog module implements static branch prediction for the Ibex processor. Here are the key aspects:\n\n## Primary Function\nThe `ibex_controller` module manages instruction flow and processor state transitions through a finite state machine (FSM) with states including RESET, DECODE, FLUSH, IRQ_TAKEN, and debug-related states.\n\n## Core Responsibilities\n\n**Exception & Interrupt Handling**: The module prioritizes and processes various exceptions (illegal instructions, fetch errors, ECALL, EBREAK) and interrupts (external, software, timer, and NMI).\n\n**Debug Mode Management**: Supports debug entry through external requests, single-step mode, or hardware breakpoints (trigger matches).\n\n**Pipeline Control**: Manages instruction validity, stalls, and pipeline flushes through signals like `halt_if`, `flush_id`, and `retain_id`.\n\n**PC Selection**: Routes program counter updates through a multiplexer supporting boot, normal execution, exception, and return-from-exception paths.\n\n## Notable Features\n\n- Configurable writeback stage and branch predictor support via parameters\n- Memory ECC error handling as an internal NMI source\n- Multi-cycle instruction awareness with stall management\n- Exception prioritization ensuring correct handling when multiple occur simultaneously\n- Performance monitoring outputs for jumps and taken branches\n\nThe module serves as the orchestrator coordinating all control flow decisions in the processor.\n\n[Binary content (application/octet-stream, 37.2KB) also saved to /home/builder/.claude/projects/-app/adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32/tool-results/webfetch-1777901647474-camso2.bin]"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32","uuid":"4998992d-7e1c-4dd7-9387-5f5953dcd84c","timestamp":"2026-05-04T13:34:12.362Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":38079,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Ibex Controller Module Analysis\n\nThis SystemVerilog code implements the main control unit for the Ibex RISC-V processor core. Here are the key features:\n\n## Core Functionality\nThe counter supports:\n- **Increment operation** via `counter_inc_i` signal\n- **Write capability** for both lower (`counter_we_i`) and upper (`counterh_we_i`) 32-bit halves\n- **Configurable width** through the `CounterWidth` parameter (default 32 bits)\n- **64-bit output** regardless of internal width\n\n## Notable Design Aspects\n\n**DSP Optimization**: The module includes Xilinx FPGA-specific logic that attempts to infer 48-bit DSPs when the counter width is less than 49 bits. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YHhrMa4A1u1PPxgKN9pQjW","type":"tool_result","content":"# Ibex Lockstep Module Overview\n\nThis SystemVerilog module implements fault detection for the Ibex RISC-V processor through lockstep redundancy. Here are the key aspects:\n\n## Core Function\nThe module instantiates a shadow copy of the processor core that runs synchronously with the main core, delayed by a configurable number of cycles (`LockstepOffset`). It continuously compares outputs to detect computational faults.\n\n## Key Features\n\n**Reset Management**: \"Upon reset, the comparison is stopped and the shadow core is reset, both immediately. A counter is started. After LockstepOffset clock cycles...The comparison is started in the following clock cycle.\"\n\n**Input Delay Pipeline**: External inputs (memory responses, interrupts, register file data) are buffered through shift registers matching the shadow core's delay offset.\n\n**Output Delay Pipeline**: Main core outputs are similarly delayed for synchronous comparison with shadow outputs.\n\n**Shadow Register File**: An additional register file instance monitors the shadow core's state, with ECC protection for fault detection at the register level.\n\n**Mismatch Detection**: The module triggers alerts when shadow and main core outputs diverge, or when internal fault checks fail.\n\n## Security Properties\nThe design includes markers for formal verification (\"SEC_CM: LOGIC.SHADOW\" and \"SEC_CM: DATA_REG_SW.GLITCH_DETECT\") indicating security-critical components requiring special analysis.\n\n[Binary content (application/octet-stream, 29.7KB) also saved to /home/builder/.claude/projects/-app/adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32/tool-results/webfetch-1777901753585-5xqt6a.bin]"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32","uuid":"918cc803-792e-477c-afe7-0719a6987ed1","timestamp":"2026-05-04T13:36:01.408Z","tool_use_result":{"bytes":30421,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# Ibex Lockstep Module Overview\n\nThis SystemVerilog module implements fault detection for the Ibex RISC-V processor through lockstep redundancy. Here are the key aspects:\n\n## Core Functionality\n\nThe module performs access control checking across multiple channels (typically instruction-side and data-side) against configurable PMP regions. It supports:\n\n- **PMP Modes**: OFF, NA4 (4-byte NAPOT), NAPOT (power-of-two), and TOR (top-of-range)\n- **Access Types**: Read, write, and execute requests\n- **Privilege Levels**: Machine (M), Supervisor (S), and User (U) modes\n\n## Smepmp Support\n\nThe implementation includes \"Machine-mode-only Locked (MML)\" semantics from the Smepmp extension, enabling more granular access control. The module orchestrates the main core, register file, instruction cache, and optional lockstep redundancy.\n\n## Key Components\n\n**Core Instantiation**: The design instantiates `ibex_core`, which handles instruction execution, memory interfaces, and interrupt handling.\n\n**Register File Options**: Three configurable register file implementations are supported: flip-flop based, FPGA-optimized, and latch-based designs.\n\n**Instruction Cache**: Optional multi-way cached instruction memory with ECC protection and scrambling support when enabled.\n\n**Lockstep Redundancy**: A shadow core (`ibex_lockstep`) can be instantiated for SecureIbex configurations to detect faults through instruction comparison.\n\n## Notable Features\n\n- **Memory ECC**: Supports error correction codes on data and instruction buses\n- **Scrambling**: Optional encryption of instruction cache contents using PRINCE cipher\n- **Clock Gating**: Power-efficient gating that respects interrupts and debug requests\n- **Multi-bit Encoding**: Security-critical signals use MuBi (Multi-bit) encoding to detect corruption\n- **RISC-V Formal Interface**: Conditional RVFI output for formal verification\n\n## Security Mechanisms\n\nThe design implements several hardening features when `SecureIbex` is enabled: dummy instruction injection, register file ECC, and instruction cache integrity checking with alert generation on multi-bit encoding errors.\n\n[Binary content (application/octet-stream, 54.9KB) also saved to /home/builder/.claude/projects/-app/adf666fe-3c05-4961-bda0-e415f5457a32/tool-results/webfetch-1777901823921-vz3u6g.bin]","durationMs":6386,"url":"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/lowRISC/ibex@master/rtl/ibex_top.sv"}}

implements the **writeback stage** of the Ibex CPU pipeline, an optional third stage responsible for writing data back to the register file.\n\n## Key Functionality\n\nThe module operates in two modes based on the `WritebackStage` parameter:\n\n**With Writeback Stage (WritebackStage = 1):**\n- Buffers instruction data and waits for completion\n- Handles load/store instructions by awaiting LSU responses\n- Non-load/store instructions complete immediately\n- Forwards register data to resolve data hazards in earlier pipeline stages\n- Tracks instruction retirement metrics, accounting for LSU errors\n\n**Without Writeback Stage (WritebackStage = 0):**\n- Acts as a \"passthrough,\" directly writing to the register file\n- Maintains constant readiness to prevent pipeline stalls\n- Simplifies
